People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… what finally made me come in is something that’s been happening at work, and it scared me enough that I couldn’t keep telling myself it was just stress or the equipment acting up.

I work in surveillance, so I’m in this room with all the monitors, and there are long quiet stretches where it’s just me watching the screens. And sometimes, even when I know the channels are muted — like I’ve checked them, I’ve actually looked and made sure — I hear voices coming through the monitor speakers. Not loud like someone yelling, but clear enough that I can make out words. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”

And I know how that sounds. I do. That’s why I haven’t really wanted to say it out loud. But no one else seems to hear it, and it happens when everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll sit there staring at the screens trying to figure out if I missed some audio feed or if there’s interference or something, but… it feels like it’s directed right at me. That’s what frightened me. I don’t feel like I can just ignore it anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s mostly at work. That’s where it’s been happening, in the surveillance room, during those really quiet stretches when I’m alone with the monitors and it gets so still that every little hum from the equipment stands out.

I don’t mean I’m listening to an open feed by mistake — I check. I’ll look at the controls, make sure the channels are muted, sometimes more than once, because I’m trying to prove to myself there’s some ordinary explanation. But then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway, like a voice just cutting through the quiet and saying my name or saying something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”

And the part that really gets to me is that no one else reacts. If someone comes in later, they didn’t hear anything. And when I check the setup, everything looks the way it should. So to answer you… not really in the same way outside of work, at least that’s not what made me come here. It’s the voices there, when everything is supposed to be muted, that scared me.

If someone comes in later, they didn’t hear anything. And when I check the setup, everything looks the way it should. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They don’t usually tell me to do something exactly, not like a command. It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.

Like I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”

So it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.

hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I feel like this heavy gray mood settles over me before I even get out of bed, and I can tear up in my car before shift over nothing more than seeing the casino lights.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to salsa class, softball, building PCs, and having people over for board games, but lately I just ignore the texts and sit at home like none of it can reach me.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: At work I catch myself staring at a report or a camera grid for too long before I can make my hands move, and people have started asking me to repeat myself because my answers come out so slow.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, walking the parking garage or cooking a simple dinner feels like I am carrying a weighted vest I never get to take off.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call like proof that I am careless and dangerous, and part of me believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I do not have some detailed plan, but I keep thinking everyone at work and at home would be safer and less burdened if I just did not wake up or if I disappeared for good.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before this stretch of feeling hopeless and guilty after the surveillance lead change, I had never heard accusations through equipment or felt so certain I was dangerous to everyone. The voices and those beliefs only seem to be part of this same low period.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I have already made mistakes on shift paperwork and had to step back from activities and friends because I cannot keep up the way I used to. At home I mostly just get through the basics instead of cooking, hosting people, or taking care of projects.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about four months, since I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 44 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally made me come in is something that’s been happening at work, and it scared me enough that I couldn’t keep telling myself it was just stress or the equipment acting up.\n\nI work in surveillance, so I’m in this room with all the monitors, and there are long quiet stretches where it’s just me watching the screens. And sometimes, even when I know the channels are muted — like I’ve checked them, I’ve actually looked and made sure — I hear voices coming through the monitor speakers. Not loud like someone yelling, but clear enough that I can make out words. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd I know how that sounds. I do. That’s why I haven’t really wanted to say it out loud. But no one else seems to hear it, and it happens when everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll sit there staring at the screens trying to figure out if I missed some audio feed or if there’s interference or something, but… it feels like it’s directed right at me. That’s what frightened me. I don’t feel like I can just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work. That’s where it’s been happening, in the surveillance room, during those really quiet stretches when I’m alone with the monitors and it gets so still that every little hum from the equipment stands out.\n\nI don’t mean I’m listening to an open feed by mistake — I check. I’ll look at the controls, make sure the channels are muted, sometimes more than once, because I’m trying to prove to myself there’s some ordinary explanation. But then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway, like a voice just cutting through the quiet and saying my name or saying something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd the part that really gets to me is that no one else reacts. If someone comes in later, they didn’t hear anything. And when I check the setup, everything looks the way it should. So to answer you… not really in the same way outside of work, at least that’s not what made me come here. It’s the voices there, when everything is supposed to be muted, that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear through the monitor speakers ever tell you to do something, or are they mostly criticizing you or making statements?\n\nPatient: They don’t usually tell me to do something exactly, not like a command. It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.

It happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”

And no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.

At home I mostly just get through the basics instead of cooking, hosting people, or taking care of projects.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about four months, since I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 44 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work. That’s where it’s been happening, in the surveillance room, during those really quiet stretches when I’m alone with the monitors and it gets so still that every little hum from the equipment stands out.\n\nI don’t mean I’m listening to an open feed by mistake — I check. I’ll look at the controls, make sure the channels are muted, sometimes more than once, because I’m trying to prove to myself there’s some ordinary explanation. But then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway, like a voice just cutting through the quiet and saying my name or saying something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd the part that really gets to me is that no one else reacts. If someone comes in later, they didn’t hear anything. And when I check the setup, everything looks the way it should. So to answer you… not really in the same way outside of work, at least that’s not what made me come here. It’s the voices there, when everything is supposed to be muted, that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear through the monitor speakers ever tell you to do something, or are they mostly criticizing you or making statements?\n\nPatient: They don’t usually tell me to do something exactly, not like a command. It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.

But then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.

So… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.

It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.

When I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.

And I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?

So aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.

make my hands move, and people have started asking me to repeat myself because my answers come out so slow.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, walking the parking garage or cooking a simple dinner feels like I am carrying a weighted vest I never get to take off.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call like proof that I am careless and dangerous, and part of me believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I do not have some detailed plan, but I keep thinking everyone at work and at home would be safer and less burdened if I just did not wake up or if I disappeared for good.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before this stretch of feeling hopeless and guilty after the surveillance lead change, I had never heard accusations through equipment or felt so certain I was dangerous to everyone. The voices and those beliefs only seem to be part of this same low period.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I have already made mistakes on shift paperwork and had to step back from activities and friends because I cannot keep up the way I used to. At home I mostly just get through the basics instead of cooking, hosting people, or taking care of projects.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about four months, since I was moved into the lead casino surveillance role.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 44 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.\n\nAnd I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?\n\nSo aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.

And it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I’ll be driving in for my shift and see the casino lights, and sometimes I’ll just start tearing up in the car. Not because something new happened right then, not because anyone said anything to me. Just seeing the place, knowing I have to go in, knowing I’m going to sit there under those lights and monitors… it feels like too much.

So yes, sad and hopeless is probably fair. It’s like the day is already ruined before it starts, and I don’t even know how to explain that without sounding dramatic.

I’ll look at the controls, make sure the channels are muted, sometimes more than once, because I’m trying to prove to myself there’s some ordinary explanation. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.\n\nAnd it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I don’t… I don’t think so. Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”

That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.\n\nAnd I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?\n\nSo aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.\n\nAnd it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I’ll be driving in for my shift and see the casino lights, and sometimes I’ll just start tearing up in the car. Not because something new happened right then, not because anyone said anything to me. Just seeing the place, knowing I have to go in, knowing I’m going to sit there under those lights and monitors… it feels like too much.\n\nSo yes, sad and hopeless is probably fair. It’s like the day is already ruined before it starts, and I don’t even know how to explain that without sounding dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses or traumatic events in your life recently or in the past?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”\n\nI keep trying to think if there’s something I’m minimizing, because when you ask it that way I feel like maybe there’s supposed to be an answer. But no, I haven’t had some major loss recently. My life has had stressful things, like anybody’s, but I can’t honestly say there’s been one big traumatic event that explains all of this.\n\nUnless you mean work stress in general, or just years of being responsible for watching everything and worrying you missed something. But I don’t know if that counts as trauma. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. So… no, I guess not anything significant that I can identify.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had times in my life where I was stressed or down, sure, and I can be hard on myself, probably more than I should be. But before this whole stretch — after the change with the surveillance lead and everything started feeling hopeless and guilty all the time — I had never heard accusations coming through equipment like that.

I’d never had it feel like the intercom or the monitors or whatever was… saying things about me. And I’d never felt so certain, in that awful way, that I was dangerous to everyone around me or that I couldn’t be trusted. That part feels new, and it seems tied up with this same period where I’ve been so low.

So no, I don’t remember anything like voices before this. Not in childhood, not earlier in adulthood, not even at other stressful jobs. This feels like it came with this particular low point, if that makes sense.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I do. I don’t always have a clear “this is what I did” kind of reason, which almost makes it worse, because then I start searching for one.

At work especially, I’ll replay things over and over in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I didn’t call something in fast enough, every moment where I think maybe I looked away too long or didn’t notice something I should have. And then it stops feeling like normal mistakes or normal human limits. It starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or that I’m dangerous to have in that job.

And if anything goes wrong on my watch, even if I don’t know that I caused it, part of me feels like I deserve whatever punishment comes from that. Like I should be blamed. Like if someone got hurt or something bad happened, it would somehow be because I wasn’t careful enough.

I know saying it out loud sounds extreme, but inside it doesn’t feel extreme. It feels like… like I’m waiting to be found out for being irresponsible, even when I can’t point to one specific thing.

So to answer you… not really in the same way outside of work, at least that’s not what made me come here. It’s the voices there, when everything is supposed to be muted, that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear through the monitor speakers ever tell you to do something, or are they mostly criticizing you or making statements?\n\nPatient: They don’t usually tell me to do something exactly, not like a command. It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.\n\nAnd I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?\n\nSo aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.\n\nAnd it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I’ll be driving in for my shift and see the casino lights, and sometimes I’ll just start tearing up in the car. Not because something new happened right then, not because anyone said anything to me. Just seeing the place, knowing I have to go in, knowing I’m going to sit there under those lights and monitors… it feels like too much.\n\nSo yes, sad and hopeless is probably fair. It’s like the day is already ruined before it starts, and I don’t even know how to explain that without sounding dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses or traumatic events in your life recently or in the past?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”\n\nI keep trying to think if there’s something I’m minimizing, because when you ask it that way I feel like maybe there’s supposed to be an answer. But no, I haven’t had some major loss recently. My life has had stressful things, like anybody’s, but I can’t honestly say there’s been one big traumatic event that explains all of this.\n\nUnless you mean work stress in general, or just years of being responsible for watching everything and worrying you missed something. But I don’t know if that counts as trauma. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. So… no, I guess not anything significant that I can identify.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience like this before, where you heard voices or had other unusual perceptions?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had times in my life where I was stressed or down, sure, and I can be hard on myself, probably more than I should be. But before this whole stretch — after the change with the surveillance lead and everything started feeling hopeless and guilty all the time — I had never heard accusations coming through equipment like that.\n\nI’d never had it feel like the intercom or the monitors or whatever was… saying things about me. And I’d never felt so certain, in that awful way, that I was dangerous to everyone around me or that I couldn’t be trusted. That part feels new, and it seems tied up with this same period where I’ve been so low.\n\nSo no, I don’t remember anything like voices before this. Not in childhood, not earlier in adulthood, not even at other stressful jobs. This feels like it came with this particular low point, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something, even if you're not sure what it is?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t always have a clear “this is what I did” kind of reason, which almost makes it worse, because then I start searching for one.\n\nAt work especially, I’ll replay things over and over in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I didn’t call something in fast enough, every moment where I think maybe I looked away too long or didn’t notice something I should have. And then it stops feeling like normal mistakes or normal human limits. It starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or that I’m dangerous to have in that job.\n\nAnd if anything goes wrong on my watch, even if I don’t know that I caused it, part of me feels like I deserve whatever punishment comes from that. Like I should be blamed. Like if someone got hurt or something bad happened, it would somehow be because I wasn’t careful enough.\n\nI know saying it out loud sounds extreme, but inside it doesn’t feel extreme. It feels like… like I’m waiting to be found out for being irresponsible, even when I can’t point to one specific thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”\n\nI keep trying to think if there’s something I’m minimizing, because when you ask it that way I feel like maybe there’s supposed to be an answer. But no, I haven’t had some major loss recently. My life has had stressful things, like anybody’s, but I can’t honestly say there’s been one big traumatic event that explains all of this.\n\nUnless you mean work stress in general, or just years of being responsible for watching everything and worrying you missed something. But I don’t know if that counts as trauma. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. So… no, I guess not anything significant that I can identify.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience like this before, where you heard voices or had other unusual perceptions?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had times in my life where I was stressed or down, sure, and I can be hard on myself, probably more than I should be. But before this whole stretch — after the change with the surveillance lead and everything started feeling hopeless and guilty all the time — I had never heard accusations coming through equipment like that.\n\nI’d never had it feel like the intercom or the monitors or whatever was… saying things about me. And I’d never felt so certain, in that awful way, that I was dangerous to everyone around me or that I couldn’t be trusted. That part feels new, and it seems tied up with this same period where I’ve been so low.\n\nSo no, I don’t remember anything like voices before this. Not in childhood, not earlier in adulthood, not even at other stressful jobs. This feels like it came with this particular low point, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something, even if you're not sure what it is?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t always have a clear “this is what I did” kind of reason, which almost makes it worse, because then I start searching for one.\n\nAt work especially, I’ll replay things over and over in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I didn’t call something in fast enough, every moment where I think maybe I looked away too long or didn’t notice something I should have. And then it stops feeling like normal mistakes or normal human limits. It starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or that I’m dangerous to have in that job.\n\nAnd if anything goes wrong on my watch, even if I don’t know that I caused it, part of me feels like I deserve whatever punishment comes from that. Like I should be blamed. Like if someone got hurt or something bad happened, it would somehow be because I wasn’t careful enough.\n\nI know saying it out loud sounds extreme, but inside it doesn’t feel extreme. It feels like… like I’m waiting to be found out for being irresponsible, even when I can’t point to one specific thing.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel guilty or worthless all the time, or does it mostly happen when you're hearing the voices or thinking about work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 44 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Worthlessness_Guilt\nmanifestation: \"I keep replaying every missed camera angle or delayed call like proof that I am careless and dangerous, and part of me believes I deserve to be punished if anything goes wrong on my watch.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s… it’s more than just when I’m hearing something, I think. The voices or the work stuff make it sharper, like it gets louder in my head, but the guilt is kind of there most of the time now.

I keep going back over things, especially from work — like every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before calling something in, every moment where I think I should’ve noticed more. And I don’t just think, “Oh, that was a mistake.” It turns into, “That proves I’m careless,” or “I’m dangerous to have watching anything important.”

So when I’m thinking about work, it gets really intense. But even away from work, it lingers. I’ll be doing something else and suddenly I’m back there in my head, replaying it again, trying to figure out what I missed. And if anything goes wrong on my watch, even if nobody has said it was my fault, part of me feels like I deserve to be punished for it. Like I should be held responsible because I should have prevented it somehow.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally made me come in is something that’s been happening at work, and it scared me enough that I couldn’t keep telling myself it was just stress or the equipment acting up.\n\nI work in surveillance, so I’m in this room with all the monitors, and there are long quiet stretches where it’s just me watching the screens. And sometimes, even when I know the channels are muted — like I’ve checked them, I’ve actually looked and made sure — I hear voices coming through the monitor speakers. Not loud like someone yelling, but clear enough that I can make out words. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd I know how that sounds. I do. That’s why I haven’t really wanted to say it out loud. But no one else seems to hear it, and it happens when everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll sit there staring at the screens trying to figure out if I missed some audio feed or if there’s interference or something, but… it feels like it’s directed right at me. That’s what frightened me. I don’t feel like I can just ignore it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work. That’s where it’s been happening, in the surveillance room, during those really quiet stretches when I’m alone with the monitors and it gets so still that every little hum from the equipment stands out.\n\nI don’t mean I’m listening to an open feed by mistake — I check. I’ll look at the controls, make sure the channels are muted, sometimes more than once, because I’m trying to prove to myself there’s some ordinary explanation. But then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway, like a voice just cutting through the quiet and saying my name or saying something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd the part that really gets to me is that no one else reacts. If someone comes in later, they didn’t hear anything. And when I check the setup, everything looks the way it should. So to answer you… not really in the same way outside of work, at least that’s not what made me come here. It’s the voices there, when everything is supposed to be muted, that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear through the monitor speakers ever tell you to do something, or are they mostly criticizing you or making statements?\n\nPatient: They don’t usually tell me to do something exactly, not like a command. It’s more… accusing me, I guess. Criticizing me, or making these statements that feel like they’re aimed right at me.\n\nLike I’ll be in the surveillance room and everything is quiet, and I’ve already checked that the channels are muted — sometimes I check more than once because I’m trying to catch myself being wrong, you know? And then I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers anyway. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nSo it’s not really, “Go do this” or “Do that.” It’s more like they’re saying I’ve already failed, or that something bad is going to happen because I didn’t notice something. That’s the part that scares me, because it feels so specific, and no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things that aren't there, like shadows or figures, especially when you're in the surveillance room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve seen, like, an actual figure standing there or anything I could point to and say, “That wasn’t real.” The thing that really scared me enough to come in was the voices.\n\nIt happens during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when I’m alone with all the monitors and everything is supposed to be muted. I’ll check the channels — sometimes I check them over and over — and then I still hear something through the monitor speakers. Not like static or a garbled sound, but words. My name, or things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nAnd no one else hears it. That’s the part that makes me feel… I don’t know, ashamed, I guess, and scared. Because I keep wanting it to be some technical issue, but when every channel is muted and nobody else reacts, I don’t know what to do with that. So, no, it’s not really shadows or figures that brought me here. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.\n\nAnd I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?\n\nSo aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.\n\nAnd it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I’ll be driving in for my shift and see the casino lights, and sometimes I’ll just start tearing up in the car. Not because something new happened right then, not because anyone said anything to me. Just seeing the place, knowing I have to go in, knowing I’m going to sit there under those lights and monitors… it feels like too much.\n\nSo yes, sad and hopeless is probably fair. It’s like the day is already ruined before it starts, and I don’t even know how to explain that without sounding dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses or traumatic events in your life recently or in the past?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”\n\nI keep trying to think if there’s something I’m minimizing, because when you ask it that way I feel like maybe there’s supposed to be an answer. But no, I haven’t had some major loss recently. My life has had stressful things, like anybody’s, but I can’t honestly say there’s been one big traumatic event that explains all of this.\n\nUnless you mean work stress in general, or just years of being responsible for watching everything and worrying you missed something. But I don’t know if that counts as trauma. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. So… no, I guess not anything significant that I can identify.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience like this before, where you heard voices or had other unusual perceptions?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had times in my life where I was stressed or down, sure, and I can be hard on myself, probably more than I should be. But before this whole stretch — after the change with the surveillance lead and everything started feeling hopeless and guilty all the time — I had never heard accusations coming through equipment like that.\n\nI’d never had it feel like the intercom or the monitors or whatever was… saying things about me. And I’d never felt so certain, in that awful way, that I was dangerous to everyone around me or that I couldn’t be trusted. Not in childhood, not earlier in adulthood, not even at other stressful jobs. This feels like it came with this particular low point, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something, even if you're not sure what it is?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t always have a clear “this is what I did” kind of reason, which almost makes it worse, because then I start searching for one.\n\nAt work especially, I’ll replay things over and over in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I didn’t call something in fast enough, every moment where I think maybe I looked away too long or didn’t notice something I should have. Not really, no. I mean… I used to have things I actually looked forward to. Lately I just… don’t answer. People text about salsa or softball or ask if I’m coming, or someone says we should do a game night, and I just let it sit there. And I’d have people over for board games sometimes, which sounds small, but I really did enjoy setting it up, picking games, making snacks, all of that.\n\nLately I just… don’t answer. People text about salsa or softball or ask if I’m coming, or someone says we should do a game night, and I just let it sit there. I see the message, and I know the old version of me would’ve responded, but now it’s like there’s this wall between me and it. I just stay home. It’s the voices through the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices you hear through the monitor speakers are real, or could they be some kind of mistake or misperception on your part?\n\nPatient: I keep going back and forth on that, honestly. Part of me wants very badly for it to be a mistake — like a wiring issue, or some feed I didn’t mute correctly, or my brain filling in sounds because the room gets so quiet. I check the channels because I’m trying to prove it’s something ordinary.\n\nBut then it happens when every channel is muted. I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers clearly enough that it feels real in the moment, like someone is saying my name or saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything. That’s what scares me.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I know it doesn’t make sense if everything is muted and nobody else reacts. But when I’m sitting there and I hear it, it doesn’t feel like a vague misperception. It feels like it’s coming through the speakers. That’s why I came in, because I can’t tell myself anymore that it’s just nothing.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel emotionally when you hear these voices, aside from being scared and ashamed?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s this heavy feeling that I’m bad at the core of it, not just that I made a mistake. Like the voices hit something that’s already there, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I hear them, I start replaying everything from my shift in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I hesitated before making a call, every moment where I looked away for even a second. And it all starts to feel like evidence. Like, see, Ruth, you are careless. You are dangerous. You shouldn’t be trusted with people’s safety.\n\nAnd I know people make mistakes at work, I know that logically, but emotionally it doesn’t feel like that. It feels like if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be my fault in this total, unforgivable way. Sometimes part of me even feels like I’d deserve to be punished for it, because if people got hurt because I missed something, then why should I get to just go home and act normal?\n\nSo aside from scared and ashamed… I guess I feel guilty. Worthless. Like I’m already guilty of something, even if I can’t point to exactly what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or hopeless when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s not only when the voices happen. Most days I feel like… like this heavy gray mood has already settled on me before I even get out of bed. I’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, before I’ve gone to work or heard anything, it’s just there.\n\nAnd it can come over me in these ordinary moments too. I’ll be driving in for my shift and see the casino lights, and sometimes I’ll just start tearing up in the car. Not because something new happened right then, not because anyone said anything to me. Just seeing the place, knowing I have to go in, knowing I’m going to sit there under those lights and monitors… it feels like too much.\n\nSo yes, sad and hopeless is probably fair. It’s like the day is already ruined before it starts, and I don’t even know how to explain that without sounding dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses or traumatic events in your life recently or in the past?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not in the way people usually mean, like a recent death or an assault or some huge accident that I can point to and say, “That’s when everything changed.”\n\nI keep trying to think if there’s something I’m minimizing, because when you ask it that way I feel like maybe there’s supposed to be an answer. But no, I haven’t had some major loss recently. My life has had stressful things, like anybody’s, but I can’t honestly say there’s been one big traumatic event that explains all of this.\n\nUnless you mean work stress in general, or just years of being responsible for watching everything and worrying you missed something. I don’t always have a clear “this is what I did” kind of reason, which almost makes it worse, because then I start searching for one.\n\nAt work especially, I’ll replay things over and over in my head — every camera angle I might have missed, every time I didn’t call something in fast enough, every moment where I think maybe I looked away too long or didn’t notice something I should have. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… most of the time, I think that’s fair to say. It’s not just that I’m busy or tired or forgetting to go. It’s more like the part of me that used to want those things has gone quiet.

Like salsa class — I used to actually look forward to it. I’d think about it ahead of time, and even if I was nervous or had a long day, once I was there I could feel myself sort of come alive a little. Same with softball. I wasn’t always amazing or anything, but I liked showing up, being part of it.

And building PCs used to really pull me in. I could spend hours comparing parts or watching videos or planning a setup, and it felt satisfying. Now I’ll maybe see something about it online and just scroll past it. It doesn’t hook me.

Board games too… I used to have people over and enjoy planning it, picking the game, setting snacks out, making it feel like a little event. Now when people text me about getting together, I mostly just ignore it. I see the messages sitting there, and I know I should answer, but I don’t. I just stay home.

It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t reach me anymore.
